\|{\boldsymbol{v}}_{\beta}\|\geq\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\|\left(1-\|({\boldsymbol{M}}-\beta{\boldsymbol{I}}_{k})\|\|({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+\beta{\boldsymbol{I}}_{k})^{-1}\|\right)\geq\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\|\left(1-\frac{\beta-\alpha}{\beta}\right)=\frac{\alpha}{\beta}\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\|,

which yields Equation (
49
).

Finally, Equation (
50
) is a direct consequence of Equations (
48
) and (
49
): take
{\boldsymbol{g}}
to be an isotropic Gaussian vector in
\mathbb{R}^{k}
. Equations (
48
) and (
49
) give

\beta^{-2}\left\|(\alpha^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{I}}_{k})^{-1}{\boldsymbol{g}}\right\|^{2}\leq\left\|({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{M}})^{-1}{\boldsymbol{g}}\right\|^{2}\leq\alpha^{-2}\left\|(\beta^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{I}}_{k})^{-1}{\boldsymbol{g}}\right\|^{2}.

Taking expectation over
{\boldsymbol{g}}
yields Equation (
50
).
∎

E.2
\|{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\nu}}\|

In this section we derive an upper bound on
\|{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\nu}}\|
. Recall that

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\nu}}={\boldsymbol{Q}}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}={\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}+{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty},

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\nu}}\|\leq\|{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}\|+\|{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|.

We bound those two terms separately. For the first term we use Equation (
46
):

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}=

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}\left({\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}+{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{1/2}\left({\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}+{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{1/2}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{1/2}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{1/2}\left({\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}+{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{1/2}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{1/2}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}\left({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}.

So,

\|{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}\|^{2}\leq\|{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-2}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}\|\left\|\left({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}\right\|^{2}.

Now we use Lemma
46
together with the observation that

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-2}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}\|\leq\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{-2},

\displaystyle\mu_{k}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{-1}{\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}\preceq{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}\preceq\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{-1}{\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}

to write

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{-2}\left(\mu_{k}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{-1}\right)^{-2}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})^{-1}\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}){\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})}{\mu_{k}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})^{2}}\frac{\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{2}}{\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{2}}\left\|\left(\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})^{-1}\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}){\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}\right\|^{2}.

For the
{k:\infty}
part we can just write

\|{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|\leq\|{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\|\|{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|\leq\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{-1}\|{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|.

Overall,

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\nu}}\|\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})}{\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})}\frac{\sqrt{\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}})}{\mu_{k}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})}\left\|\left(\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})^{-1}\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}){\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}\right\|

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{-1}\|{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|.

E.3
{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\perp}}}{{\vphantom{.}\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\sim}}}}}

E.3.1
Bound from below

In this section we derive a lower bound on
{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\perp}}}{{\vphantom{.}\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\sim}}}}}
. First of all, we write

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\perp}}}{{\vphantom{.}\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\sim}}}}}=

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\top}({\boldsymbol{I}}_{p}-{\boldsymbol{Q}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}){\boldsymbol{\mu}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}^{\top}({\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}-{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}){\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}^{\top}({\boldsymbol{I}}_{p-k}-{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}){\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle 2{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}.

We see that this decomposition has 3 terms: energy in the spiked part, energy in the tail, and the cross term. We expect the positive contribution to come from
{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}^{\top}({\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}-{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}){\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}+{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{I}}_{p-k}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}
, the other terms will be upper bounded in absolute value and subtracted from the lower bound. The last term (the cross term) is a bit tricky, because bounding it separately leads to a potentially vacuous bound. The approach we take here is to bound it in terms of the quantities from the first two terms, and then bounding those quantities.

Due to Equation (
47
) the first term becomes

{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}^{\top}\left({\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}+{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}.

Now let’s apply a similar transformation to the cross-term: we use Equation (
46
) to write

\displaystyle\left|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}^{\top}\left({\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}+{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}^{\top}\left({\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}+{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}\right)^{-1/2}\left({\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}+{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}\right)^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle w{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}^{\top}\left({\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}+{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle w^{-1}\left\|\left({\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}+{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}\right)^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle w{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}^{\top}\left({\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}+{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}+w^{-1}\|{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2},

where we introduced an arbitrary scalar
w>0
when we used AM-GM inequality. In the last line we also used the following fact:

\left\|\left({\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}+{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}\right)^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1/2}\right\|\leq 1.

Indeed, the matrix
{\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}+{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}
is larger than the matrix
{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1/2}({\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1/2})^{\top}
in the PSD sense.

We take
w=0.25
. So far we obtained that

{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\perp}}}{{\vphantom{.}\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\sim}}}}}\geq\frac{1}{2}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}^{\top}\left({\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}+{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}+\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2}-9\|{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2},

where we also used

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}^{\top}({\boldsymbol{I}}_{p-k}-{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}){\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}=

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2}-{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}^{\top}\underbrace{{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}}_{\preceq{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2}-\|{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2}.

Now we just need to bound
{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}^{\top}\left({\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}+{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}
from below and
\|{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2}
from above. We write

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}^{\top}\left({\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}+{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{-1/2}_{0:k}\left({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{-1}_{0:k}+{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{-1/2}_{0:k}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{-1/2}_{0:k}\left({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{-1}_{0:k}+\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{-1}\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}){\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{-1/2}_{0:k}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})^{-1}\left\|\left(\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{-1}_{0:k}+{\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}\right)^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{-1/2}_{0:k}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}\right\|^{2}.

For the term
\|{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2}
we simply do a norm-times-norm bound:

\|{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2}\leq\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{-1}\|{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2}

Combining everything together gives the bound.

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\perp}}}{{\vphantom{.}\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\sim}}}}}\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})^{-1}\left\|\left(\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{-1}_{0:k}+{\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}\right)^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{-1/2}_{0:k}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2}-9\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{-1}\|{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2}

E.3.2
Bound from above

In this section we bound
{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\perp}}}{{\vphantom{.}\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\sim}}}}}
from above. This is easier than bounding it from below. Indeed, recall the decomposition from the previous section:

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\perp}}}{{\vphantom{.}\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\sim}}}}}=

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}^{\top}({\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}-{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}){\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}^{\top}({\boldsymbol{I}}_{p-k}-{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}){\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle 2{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}.

For the first term we had

{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}^{\top}({\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}-{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}){\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}={\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}^{\top}\left({\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}+{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}.

and for the cross-term

\displaystyle\left|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle w{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}^{\top}\left({\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}+{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}+w^{-1}\|{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2},

for any
w>0
. Here we will take
w=1
. For the second term we simply write

{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}^{\top}({\boldsymbol{I}}_{p-k}-{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}){\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\leq\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2}.

Combining everything together, we get

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\perp}}}{{\vphantom{.}\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\sim}}}}}\leq

\displaystyle 3{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}^{\top}\left({\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}+{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}+\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2}+2\|{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 3{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1/2}\left({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}+\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2}+2\|{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 3\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})\mu_{k}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})^{-1}\left\|\left(\mu_{k}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})^{-1}\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}){\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{-1}_{0:k}+{\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}\right)^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{-1/2}_{0:k}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2}+2\|{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2}.

E.4
\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\perp}}}{{\vphantom{.}\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\sim}}}}}\|_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2}

The quantity
\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\perp}}}{{\vphantom{.}\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\sim}}}}}\|_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2}
is exactly the bias term from
Tsigler and Bartlett (2023)
: indeed, plug in
{\boldsymbol{\mu}}
instead of their
\theta^{*}
,
{\boldsymbol{Q}}
instead of their
X
,
{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}
instead of their
\Sigma
, and
{\boldsymbol{A}}
instead of their
A
. We could, in principle, just borrow an algebraic bound from their Lemma 28. However, for the sake of completeness, and because we would like a bound in a slightly different form, we do our own derivation here.

As before, we start with the first
k
components and use Lemma
45
:

\displaystyle\left\|[{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\perp}}}{{\vphantom{.}\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\sim}}}}}]_{0:k}\right\|^{2}_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}}/2

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|({\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}-{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}){\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}\right\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\right\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|\left({\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}+{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}\right\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}}^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left\|\left({\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}+{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\right\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|\left({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left\|\left({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\mu_{k}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{-1}\right)^{-2}\left\|\left(\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})^{-1}\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}){\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{2}\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})^{-2}\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top})\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{-2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\right\|^{2},

where in the last transition we used Lemma
46
and the following observation:

\left\|\left({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}\right)^{-1}\right\|\leq\mu_{k}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})^{-1}\leq\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})\mu_{k}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})^{-1}.

When it comes to the rest of the components, we write

\displaystyle[{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\perp}}}{{\vphantom{.}\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\sim}}}}}]_{k:\infty}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}-{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}-{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}-{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}-{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}-{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}\left({\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}+{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}-{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}-{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}\left({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k},

which yields

\displaystyle\|[{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\perp}}}{{\vphantom{.}\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\sim}}}}}]_{k:\infty}\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{k:\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{k:\infty}}+\|{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}^{\top}\|^{1/2}\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}})^{-1}\|{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}^{\top}\|^{1/2}\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{-1}\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})^{1/2}\left\|\left({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{k:\infty}}+\|{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}^{\top}\|^{1/2}\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{-1}\|{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}^{\top}\|^{1/2}\frac{\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{-1}\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})^{1/2}}{\mu_{k}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{-1}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})^{-1}\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}){\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}\right\|,

where we used Lemma
46
and the fact that
\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}})\geq\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})
in the last transition. Combining everything together and using the inequality
(a+b+c)^{2}\leq 3(a^{2}+b^{2}+c^{2})
yields the final bound.

E.5
\text{tr}({\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{Q}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1})

The quantity
\text{tr}({\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{Q}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1})
is exactly the variance term from
Tsigler and Bartlett (2023)
: as for the bias term, plug in
{\boldsymbol{Q}}
instead of their
X
,
{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}
instead of their
\Sigma
, and
{\boldsymbol{A}}
instead of their
A
. As before, we could in principle use their algebraic decomposition from their Lemma 27, but we provide our own derivation because we want to obtain the bound in a slightly different form.

\displaystyle\text{tr}({\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}[{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k},{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}]{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}[{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k},{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}]^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\text{tr}({\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1})+\text{tr}({\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\text{tr}\left({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}\left({\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}+{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}\left({\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}+{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\text{tr}({\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\text{tr}\left({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}\left({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}\right)^{-2}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\text{tr}({\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1})

Now let’s bound these two terms separately. First, recall that for any PSD matrices
{\boldsymbol{M}}_{1}
and
{\boldsymbol{M}}_{2}
the following holds:
\text{tr}({\boldsymbol{M}}_{1}{\boldsymbol{M}}_{2})\leq\|{\boldsymbol{M}}_{1}\|\text{tr}({\boldsymbol{M}}_{2})
. We use this to bound the first term as follows:

\displaystyle\text{tr}\left({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}\left({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}\right)^{-2}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{-2}\text{tr}\left({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}\left({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}\right)^{-2}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{-2}\text{tr}\left(\left({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}\right)^{-2}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{-2}\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})\text{tr}\left(\left({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}\right)^{-2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{-2}\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})}{\mu_{k}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})^{2}\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{-2}}\text{tr}\left(\left(\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})^{-1}\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}){\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}\right)^{-2}\right),

where we used Lemma
46
in the last transition.

When it comes to the second term, we simply write

\displaystyle\text{tr}({\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1})\leq\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{-2}\text{tr}\left({\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}^{\top}\right).

E.6
\|{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{Q}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\|

In
Tsigler and Bartlett (2023)
the deviations of the variance term in noise were dealt with in the following way: Hanson-Wright inequality states that a quadratic form
{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{M}}{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}
, where
{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}
is a vector with i.i.d. centered sub-Gaussian components concentrates around
\text{tr}({\boldsymbol{M}})
, with deviations being composed of a sub-Gaussian tail controlled by
\|{\boldsymbol{M}}\|_{F}
and sub-exponential tail controlled by
\|{\boldsymbol{M}}\|
. In
Tsigler and Bartlett (2023)
the latter two quantities were bounded as
\|{\boldsymbol{M}}\|_{F}^{2}\leq\text{tr}({\boldsymbol{M}})^{2}
and
\|{\boldsymbol{M}}\|\leq\text{tr}({\boldsymbol{M}})
, so only the bound on the trace of the matrix
{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{Q}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}
was required. Instead of making such step, we can bound the spectral norm separately, and then use
\|{\boldsymbol{M}}\|_{F}^{2}\leq\|{\boldsymbol{M}}\|\text{tr}({\boldsymbol{M}})
. This section shows the following:

\|{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{Q}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\|\leq\frac{\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1})^{2}}{\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1})^{2}}\frac{\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})}{\mu_{k}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})^{2}}+\frac{\|{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}^{\top}\|}{\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{2}}.

We bound the operator norm as follows: first, we decompose into two terms

{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{Q}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}={\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}.

The second term is straightforward:

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\|\|{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}^{\top}\|\|{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\|{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}^{\top}\|}{\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{2}}.

For the first term we use Equation (
46
) to write

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\|

\displaystyle=\left\|{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}\left({\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}+{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}\left({\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}+{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}\right\|

\displaystyle=\|{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}\left({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}\right)^{-2}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\left({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}\right)^{-2}\right\|\|{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}\|\|{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\lambda_{1}^{2}\wedge\left\|\left({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}\right)^{-2}\right\|\right)\|{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}\|\|{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{2}}{\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{2}}\frac{\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})}{\mu_{k}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})^{2}}\wedge\frac{\lambda_{1}^{2}\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})}{\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{2}}.

Appendix F
Randomness in covariates

Lemma 47

(Randomness in covariates)
.

Consider some
L>1
. There exists a constant
c
that only depends on
c_{B}
and
L
such that the following holds. Denote

\Lambda=\lambda+\sum_{i>k}\lambda_{i}

Assume that
k<n/c
and

\Lambda>cn\lambda_{k+1}\vee\sqrt{n\sum_{i>k}\lambda_{i}^{2}}.

Then all the following hold on the event
\mathscr{A}_{k}(L)\cap\mathscr{B}_{k}(c_{B})
:

1.

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\nu}}\|\leq

\displaystyle c\left(n^{-1/2}\left\|\left(\Lambda n^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}\right\|+\Lambda^{-1}{\sqrt{n}\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{k:\infty}}}\right);

2.

\displaystyle c{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\perp}}}{{\vphantom{.}\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\sim}}}}}\geq\frac{\Lambda}{n}\left\|\left(\Lambda n^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}\right)^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}\right\|+\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2}\geq{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\perp}}}{{\vphantom{.}\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\sim}}}}}/c;

3.

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\perp}}}{{\vphantom{.}\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\sim}}}}}\|_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2}/c\leq\Lambda^{2}n^{-2}\left\|\left(\Lambda n^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}\right\|^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\right\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{k:\infty}}^{2};

4.

\displaystyle\text{tr}({\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{Q}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1})\leq

\displaystyle c\left(n^{-1}\text{tr}\left(\left(\Lambda n^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}\right)^{-2}\right)+\Lambda^{-2}n\sum_{i>k}\lambda_{i}^{2}\right);

5.

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{Q}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\|\leq

\displaystyle c\left(\frac{1}{n}\wedge\frac{n\lambda_{1}^{2}}{\Lambda^{2}}+\frac{n\lambda_{k+1}^{2}+\sum_{i>k}\lambda_{i}^{2}}{\Lambda^{2}}\right).

Proof.

Recall that on
\mathscr{A}_{k}(L)
we have
\Lambda/L\leq\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})\leq\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})\leq L\Lambda
.

The proof is rather straightforward: we plug the bounds from the definition of the event
\mathscr{B}_{k}(c_{B})
from Section
3.2
, and the bounds on eigenvalues of
{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}
from the definition of the event
\mathscr{A}_{k}(L)
into the result of Lemma
44
. Recall that
c_{B}
is the constant from the definition of
\mathscr{B}_{k}(c_{B})
.

On
\mathscr{A}_{k}(L)\cap\mathscr{B}_{k}(c_{B})
all the following hold:

1.

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\nu}}\|\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})}{\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})}\frac{\sqrt{\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}})}{\mu_{k}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})}\left\|\left(\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})^{-1}\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}){\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}\right\|

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{-1}\|{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c_{B}^{3/2}L^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\cdot c_{B}L\left\|\left(\Lambda n^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}\right\|+c_{B}^{1/2}L\Lambda^{-1}{\sqrt{n}\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{k:\infty}}}.

Note that in the last transition we used that for a positive scalar
a<1
we can write

\left\|\left(a\Lambda n^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}\right\|\leq a^{-1}\left\|\left(\Lambda n^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}\right\|.

It is easy to see that this is correct since both matrices
{\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}
and
{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}
are diagonal. We will make such a transition several more times throughout this proof, as well as the following for
b\geq 1
:

\left\|\left(b\Lambda n^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}\right\|\geq b^{-1}\left\|\left(\Lambda n^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}\right\|.

2.

We start with the lower bound on
{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\perp}}}{{\vphantom{.}\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\sim}}}}}
:

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\perp}}}{{\vphantom{.}\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\sim}}}}}\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})^{-1}\left\|\left(\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{-1}_{0:k}+{\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}\right)^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{-1/2}_{0:k}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2}-9\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{-1}\|{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\Lambda}{2Lc_{B}n}\cdot\frac{1}{c_{B}L}\left\|\left(\Lambda n^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}\right)^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}\right\|

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2}-9Lc_{B}\Lambda^{-1}n\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2}_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{k:\infty}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\Lambda}{2L^{2}c_{B}^{2}n}\left\|\left(\Lambda n^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}\right)^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}\right\|+\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2}(1-9Lc_{B}n\lambda_{k+1}\Lambda^{-1}),

where in the last line we used that
\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2}_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{k:\infty}}\leq\lambda_{k+1}\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2}
. Note that if we take
c>18c_{B}L
in the end, then
1-9Lc_{B}n\lambda_{k+1}\Lambda^{-1}>0.5
since we assumed that
\Lambda>cn\lambda_{k+1}
.

Now, we do the upper bound:

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\perp}}}{{\vphantom{.}\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\sim}}}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 3\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})\mu_{k}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})^{-1}\left\|\left(\mu_{k}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})^{-1}\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}){\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{-1}_{0:k}+{\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}\right)^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{-1/2}_{0:k}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2}+2\|{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 3Lc_{B}\Lambda n^{-1}\cdot c_{B}L\left\|\left(\Lambda n^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{-1}_{0:k}+{\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}\right)^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{-1/2}_{0:k}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2}+2Lc_{B}\Lambda^{-1}n\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2}_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{k:\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 3L^{2}c_{B}^{2}\Lambda n^{-1}\left\|\left(\Lambda n^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{-1}_{0:k}+{\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}\right)^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{-1/2}_{0:k}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}\right\|^{2}+\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2}(1+2Lc_{B}\lambda_{k+1}\Lambda^{-1}n),

where, as before, we used
\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2}_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{k:\infty}}\leq\lambda_{k+1}\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2}
in the last line. Note that here
\Lambda>cn\lambda_{k+1}
implies that
2Lc_{B}\lambda_{k+1}\Lambda^{-1}n\leq 2Lc_{B}/c<1
for
c
large enough.

3.

The upper bound on
\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\perp}}}{{\vphantom{.}\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\sim}}}}}\|_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2}
, is very similar to the bound on the bias term in
Tsigler and Bartlett (2023)
, but has a slightly different form. We derive it below.

\displaystyle{\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\perp}}}{{\vphantom{.}\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\sim}}}}}\|_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\mu_{k}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})^{-2}\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{2}\left\|\left(\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})^{-1}\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}){\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 2\frac{\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{2}\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top})}{\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})^{2}\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{2}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 3\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{k:\infty}}^{2}+3\|{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}^{\top}\|\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{-2}\|{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 3\|{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}^{\top}\|\frac{\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{2}\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})}{\mu_{k}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})^{2}\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{2}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})^{-1}\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}){\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2c_{B}^{2}L^{2}\Lambda^{2}n^{-2}\cdot c_{B}^{2}L^{2}\left\|\left(\Lambda n^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 2L^{4}c_{B}^{3}n^{-1}\cdot c_{B}n\left\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\right\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{k:\infty}}^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 3\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{k:\infty}}^{2}+3c_{B}\left(n\lambda_{k+1}^{2}+\sum_{i>k}\lambda_{i}^{2}\right)\cdot L^{2}\Lambda^{-2}c_{B}n\left\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\right\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{k:\infty}}^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 3c_{B}\left(n\lambda_{k+1}^{2}+\sum_{i>k}\lambda_{i}^{2}\right)\cdot L^{4}c_{B}^{3}n^{-1}\cdot c_{B}^{2}L^{2}\left\|\left(\Lambda n^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\Lambda n^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}\right\|^{2}\cdot\left(2c_{B}^{4}L^{4}\Lambda^{2}n^{-2}+3c_{B}^{6}L^{6}n^{-1}\left(n\lambda_{k+1}^{2}+\sum_{i>k}\lambda_{i}^{2}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\right\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{k:\infty}}^{2}\cdot\left(2L^{4}c_{B}^{4}+3+3c_{B}^{2}L^{2}\Lambda^{-2}n\left(n\lambda_{k+1}^{2}+\sum_{i>k}\lambda_{i}^{2}\right)\right)

Recall that we imposed the assumption that

\Lambda>n\lambda_{k+1},\quad\Lambda>\sqrt{n\sum_{i>k}\lambda_{i}^{2}}.

Therefore

n\left(n\lambda_{k+1}^{2}+\sum_{i>k}\lambda_{i}^{2}\right)\leq 2\Lambda^{2}.

Plugging this inequality in and taking
c
large enough depending on
c_{B}
and
L
gives the bound.

4.

The upper bound on
\text{tr}({\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{Q}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1})
, is also very similar to the one derived in
Tsigler and Bartlett (2023)
, where it is exactly the the variance term, but has a slightly different form. We derive it below:

\displaystyle\text{tr}({\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{Q}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{2}\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})}{\mu_{k}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})^{2}\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{2}}\text{tr}\left(\left(\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})^{-1}\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}){\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}\right)^{-2}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{-2}\text{tr}({\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}^{\top})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle L^{4}c_{B}^{3}n^{-1}\cdot c_{B}L\text{tr}\left(\left(\Lambda n^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}\right)^{-2}\right)+L^{2}c_{B}\Lambda^{-2}n\sum_{i>k}\lambda_{i}^{2}.

5.

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{Q}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{2}}{\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{2}}\frac{\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})}{\mu_{k}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})^{2}}\wedge\frac{\lambda_{1}^{2}\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})}{\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{2}}+\frac{\|{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}^{\top}\|}{\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{L^{4}c_{B}^{3}}{n}\wedge\frac{\lambda_{1}^{2}c_{B}n}{L^{-2}\Lambda^{2}}+c_{B}L^{2}\Lambda^{-2}\left(n\lambda_{k+1}^{2}+\sum_{i>k}\lambda_{i}^{2}\right).

In all the cases above we see that taking
c
large enough depending on
c_{B}
and
L
yields the result.
∎

Appendix G
Proof of the main lower bound

First of all, we combine Lemma
43
with Lemma
47
to obtain high probability bounds on all the terms that appear in quantities of interest. The result is given by the following

Lemma 48

(High probability bounds on separate terms)
.

Consider some
L>1
. There exists a constant
c
that only depends on
c_{B}
and
L
and an absolute constant
c_{y}
such that the following holds. Assume that
\eta<c^{-1}
.
Assume that
k<n/c

\Lambda>cn\lambda_{k+1}\vee\sqrt{n\sum_{i>k}\lambda_{i}^{2}}.

For any
t\in(0,\sqrt{n}/c_{y})
, conditionally on the event
\mathscr{A}_{k}(L)\cap\mathscr{B}_{k}(c_{B})
, with probability is at least
1-c_{y}e^{-t^{2}/2}
over the draw of
({\boldsymbol{y}},\hat{{\boldsymbol{y}}})
all the following hold:

1.

\displaystyle|{\boldsymbol{\nu}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{y}}|\vee|{\boldsymbol{\nu}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\hat{{\boldsymbol{y}}}|\leq

\displaystyle ct\Diamond,

2.

\displaystyle|{\boldsymbol{\nu}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\Delta{\boldsymbol{y}}|\leq

\displaystyle ct\sigma_{\eta}\Diamond,

3.

\displaystyle|\Delta{\boldsymbol{y}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{y}}|\leq

\displaystyle c\sigma_{\eta}n\Lambda^{-1},

4.

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{y}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\hat{{\boldsymbol{y}}}\geq

\displaystyle c^{-1}n\Lambda^{-1},

5.

\displaystyle cn\Lambda^{-1}\geq{\boldsymbol{y}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{y}}\geq c^{-1}n\Lambda^{-1},

6.

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{Q}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\Delta{\boldsymbol{y}}\|_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle c\sigma_{\eta}^{2}(V+t^{2}\Delta V),

7.

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{Q}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{y}}\|_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle c(V+t^{2}\Delta V),

8.

\displaystyle cM\geq{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\perp}}}{{\vphantom{.}\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\sim}}}}}\geq

\displaystyle c^{-1}M,

9.

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\perp}}}{{\vphantom{.}\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\sim}}}}}\|_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\leq

\displaystyle c\Lambda\Diamond/\sqrt{n},

Proof.

Parts 1, 2, 6 and 7 can be obtained directly from the corresponding parts of Lemma
43
by plugging in the bounds from Lemma
47
. Parts 8 and 9 are exactly parts 2 and 3 of Lemma
47
. Thus, only parts 3, 4, and 5 require additional explanation, which we provide below.

First of all, note that
\|{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\|\leq\|{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}\|=\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{-1}
, since
{\boldsymbol{A}}
is larger than
{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}
w.r.t. Loewner order. Thus, on
\mathscr{A}_{k}(L)
we have

\|{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\|\leq L\Lambda^{-1},\quad\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{-1}\geq L^{-1}\Lambda^{-1},

Now let’s explain parts 3, 4, 5 starting with the corresponding parts of Lemma
43
. Denote the (absolute) constant from that lemma as
c_{1}
. In all the following we plug in the bounds on eigenvalues of
{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}
, together with
\eta<1/c
,
k<n/c
and
t<\sqrt{n}/c
. In the end of each derivation we need to take
c
large enough depending on
L
and
c_{1}
.

By Lemma
43
for every
t\in(0,\sqrt{n}/c_{1})
on
\mathscr{A}_{k}(L)\cap\mathscr{B}_{k}(c_{B})
we have with probability at least
1-c_{1}e^{-t^{2}/2}
over the draw of
({\boldsymbol{y}},\hat{{\boldsymbol{y}}})

3.

\displaystyle|\Delta{\boldsymbol{y}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{y}}|\leq

\displaystyle c_{1}\|{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\|\left(n\eta+t\sigma_{\eta}(\sqrt{n}+t)\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{1}L\Lambda(n\sigma_{\eta}+\sqrt{n}\sigma_{\eta}(\sqrt{n}+\sqrt{n})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle cn\Lambda^{-1}\sigma_{\eta}.

4.

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{y}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\hat{{\boldsymbol{y}}}\geq

\displaystyle(n-n\eta-c_{1}t\sigma_{\eta}\sqrt{n}-k)\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{-1}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle(n\eta+c_{1}t\sigma_{\eta}\sqrt{n}+c_{1}t\sqrt{n}+c_{1}t^{2})\|{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle L^{-1}\Lambda^{-1}n(1-1/c-c_{1}\sigma_{\eta}/c-1/c-L^{2}/c-c_{1}L^{2}\sigma_{\eta}/c-L^{2}c_{1}/c)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle c^{-1}n\Lambda^{-1}.

5.

cn\Lambda^{-1}\geq nL\Lambda^{-1}\geq n\|{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\|\geq{\boldsymbol{y}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{y}},

and

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{y}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{y}}\geq

\displaystyle(n-k)\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{-1}-c_{1}(t\sqrt{n}+t^{2})\|{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle(n-n/c)L^{-1}\Lambda^{-1}-c_{1}(n/c+n/c^{2})L\Lambda^{-1}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle c^{-1}n\Lambda^{-1}.

∎

See
8

Proof.

Note that increasing
c
only makes the statement weaker. From the very beginning let’s put
c
to be large enough, so that
c>1
and
\sigma_{\eta}<1
.

Recall that

\Delta V:=\frac{k\wedge 1}{n}+\frac{n\lambda_{k+1}^{2}+\sum_{i>k}\lambda_{i}^{2}}{\left(\lambda+\sum_{i>k}\lambda_{i}\right)^{2}}.

The plan is to plug in the bounds from Lemma
48
into quantities of interest, the formulas for which are given by Lemma
33
. First of all, however, we need to make sure that
S>0
. This is required to write
\|S{{\boldsymbol{w}}_{\text{\tiny{ridge}}}}\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}=S\|{{\boldsymbol{w}}_{\text{\tiny{ridge}}}}\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}
and then cancel
S
in the numerator and denominator. This is indeed the case since
S=(1+{\boldsymbol{\nu}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{y}})^{2}+{\boldsymbol{y}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{y}}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\perp}}}{{\vphantom{.}\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\sim}}}}}
, and in Lemma
48
we bound
{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\perp}}}{{\vphantom{.}\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\sim}}}}}
and
{\boldsymbol{y}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{y}}
from below by strictly positive quantities.

Let’s plug in the bounds Lemma
48
in the formulas from Lemma
33
: denote the constant from Lemma
48
as
c_{1}
and write

1.

S{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\top}{{\boldsymbol{w}}_{\text{\tiny{ridge}}}}
: recall that
{\boldsymbol{y}}_{C}
is the vector of labels of clean points:
{\boldsymbol{y}}_{C}={\boldsymbol{y}}+\Delta{\boldsymbol{y}}/2=\hat{{\boldsymbol{y}}}-\Delta{\boldsymbol{y}}/2
. Now we write

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{y}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\hat{{\boldsymbol{y}}}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\perp}}}{{\vphantom{.}\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\sim}}}}}+(1+{\boldsymbol{\nu}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{y}}){\boldsymbol{\nu}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\hat{{\boldsymbol{y}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{y}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\hat{{\boldsymbol{y}}}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\perp}}}{{\vphantom{.}\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\sim}}}}}+{\boldsymbol{\nu}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\hat{{\boldsymbol{y}}}+{\boldsymbol{\nu}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}({\boldsymbol{y}}_{C}-\Delta{\boldsymbol{y}}/2){\boldsymbol{\nu}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}({\boldsymbol{y}}_{C}+\Delta{\boldsymbol{y}}/2)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{y}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\hat{{\boldsymbol{y}}}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\perp}}}{{\vphantom{.}\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\sim}}}}}+{\boldsymbol{\nu}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\hat{{\boldsymbol{y}}}-({\boldsymbol{\nu}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\Delta{\boldsymbol{y}})^{2}/4+({\boldsymbol{\nu}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{y}}_{C})^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{y}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\hat{{\boldsymbol{y}}}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\perp}}}{{\vphantom{.}\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\sim}}}}}+{\boldsymbol{\nu}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\hat{{\boldsymbol{y}}}-({\boldsymbol{\nu}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\Delta{\boldsymbol{y}})^{2}/4

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{n}{c_{1}^{2}\Lambda}M-c_{1}t\Diamond-c_{1}^{2}t^{2}\sigma_{\eta}^{2}\Diamond^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{N}{c_{1}^{2}}-c_{1}t\Diamond-c_{1}^{2}t^{2}\sigma_{\eta}^{2}\Diamond^{2}.

Recall that by Lemma
7
we have
N\geq n\Diamond^{2}
, which yields

\frac{N}{c_{1}^{2}}-c_{1}^{2}t^{2}\sigma_{\eta}^{2}\Diamond^{2}\geq\frac{N}{c_{1}^{2}}-\frac{c_{1}^{2}}{c^{2}}n\Diamond^{2}\geq\frac{N}{2c_{1}^{2}},

where the last transition is correct if
c
is taken large enough depending on
c_{1}
. Thus, we get

{\boldsymbol{y}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\hat{{\boldsymbol{y}}}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\perp}}}{{\vphantom{.}\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\sim}}}}}+(1+{\boldsymbol{\nu}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{y}}){\boldsymbol{\nu}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\hat{{\boldsymbol{y}}}\geq\frac{N}{2c_{1}^{2}}-c_{1}t\Diamond.

2.

S\|{{\boldsymbol{w}}_{\text{\tiny{ridge}}}}\|_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}
, the first term:

\displaystyle\left[(1+|{\boldsymbol{\nu}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{y}}|)^{2}+{\boldsymbol{y}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{y}}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\perp}}}{{\vphantom{.}\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\sim}}}}}\right]\|{\boldsymbol{Q}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\Delta{\boldsymbol{y}}\|_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left[(1+c_{1}t\Diamond)^{2}+c_{1}^{2}Mn\Lambda^{-1}\right]\sqrt{c_{1}\sigma_{\eta}^{2}(V+t^{2}\Delta V)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{1}^{2.5}\left[\left(1+t\Diamond\right)^{2}+n\Lambda^{-1}M\right]\sigma_{\eta}\sqrt{V+t^{2}\Delta V}.

3.

S\|{{\boldsymbol{w}}_{\text{\tiny{ridge}}}}\|_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}
, the second term

\displaystyle\left[(1+|{\boldsymbol{\nu}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{y}}|)(1+|{\boldsymbol{\nu}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\Delta{\boldsymbol{y}}|)+|\Delta{\boldsymbol{y}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{y}}|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\perp}}}{{\vphantom{.}\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\sim}}}}}\right]\|{\boldsymbol{Q}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{y}}\|_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left[(1+c_{1}t\Diamond)(1+c_{1}\sigma_{\eta}t\Diamond)+c_{1}^{2}M\sigma_{\eta}n\Lambda^{-1}\right]\sqrt{c_{1}(V+t^{2}\Delta V)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{1}^{2.5}\Biggl[1+(1+\sigma_{\eta})t\Diamond+\sigma_{\eta}t^{2}\Diamond^{2}+n\Lambda^{-1}M\sigma_{\eta}\Biggr]\sqrt{V+t^{2}\Delta V}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2c_{1}^{3}\Biggl[1+t\Diamond+n\Lambda^{-1}M\sigma_{\eta}\Biggr]\sqrt{V+t^{2}\Delta V},

where we used that
\sigma_{\eta}<1
and
t^{2}\Diamond^{2}<n\Diamond^{2}<n\Lambda^{-1}M
in the last transition (by Lemma
7
).

4.

S\|{{\boldsymbol{w}}_{\text{\tiny{ridge}}}}\|_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}
, the third term

\displaystyle\left[{\boldsymbol{y}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{y}}+(1+|{\boldsymbol{\nu}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{y}}|)|\Delta{\boldsymbol{y}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{y}}|+{\boldsymbol{y}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{y}}|{\boldsymbol{\nu}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\Delta{\boldsymbol{y}}|\right]\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\perp}}}{{\vphantom{.}\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\sim}}}}}\|_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left[\frac{c_{1}n}{\Lambda}+(1+c_{1}t\Diamond)\frac{c_{1}n\sigma_{\eta}}{\Lambda}+\frac{c_{1}^{2}nt\sigma_{\eta}\Diamond}{\Lambda}\right]\frac{c_{1}\Lambda}{\sqrt{n}}\Diamond

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2c_{1}^{3}\sqrt{n}(1+t\sigma_{\eta}\Diamond)\Diamond,

where we used
c_{1}n\Lambda^{-1}(1+\sigma_{\eta})\leq 2c_{1}n\Lambda^{-1}
in the last line to reduce the number of terms.

Combining all the terms for
S\|{{\boldsymbol{w}}_{\text{\tiny{ridge}}}}\|_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}
, we get that for some new constant
c_{2}
that only depends on
L
and
c_{B}
under the condition that
t\leq\sqrt{n}/c_{2}
and
\eta<1/c_{2}

\displaystyle S\|{{\boldsymbol{w}}_{\text{\tiny{ridge}}}}\|_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}/c_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\left[\left(1+t\Diamond\right)^{2}+n\Lambda^{-1}M\right]\sigma_{\eta}\sqrt{V+t^{2}\Delta V}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left[1+t\Diamond+n\Lambda^{-1}M\sigma_{\eta}\right]\sqrt{V+t^{2}\Delta V}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(1+t\sigma_{\eta}\Diamond)\Diamond

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Diamond^{2}\cdot t\sigma_{\eta}(\sqrt{n}+t\sqrt{V+t^{2}\Delta V})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\Diamond\cdot\bigl(\sqrt{n}+t(1+2\sigma_{\eta})\sqrt{V+t^{2}\Delta V}\bigr)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left[1+\sigma_{\eta}+2n\Lambda^{-1}M\sigma_{\eta}\right]\sqrt{V+t^{2}\Delta V}

By Lemma
7

V\leq 2,\quad\Delta V\leq 3/n,\quad t^{2}\Delta V\leq 3.

This allows us to obtain the final bound on
S\|{{\boldsymbol{w}}_{\text{\tiny{ridge}}}}\|_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}
: plug in the following inequalities:

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}+t\sqrt{V+t^{2}\Delta V}\leq

\displaystyle(1+\sqrt{5})\sqrt{n},

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}+t(1+2\sigma_{\eta})\sqrt{V+t^{2}\Delta V}\leq

\displaystyle(1+3\sqrt{5})\sqrt{n},

\displaystyle 1+\sigma_{\eta}\leq

\displaystyle 2.

We get for some
c_{3}
that only depends on
L,c_{B}
:

S\|{{\boldsymbol{w}}_{\text{\tiny{ridge}}}}\|_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\leq c_{3}\left(\left[1+n\Lambda^{-1}M\sigma_{\eta}\right]\sqrt{V+t^{2}\Delta V}+\Diamond\sqrt{n}+t\sigma_{\eta}\Diamond^{2}\sqrt{n}\right).

(51)

Finally, note that by Lemma
7
we have
\Diamond^{2}\leq\Lambda^{-1}M\sqrt{n\Delta V}
, so

t\sigma_{\eta}\Diamond^{2}\sqrt{n}\leq t\sigma_{\eta}M\Lambda^{-1}n\sqrt{\Delta V}=\sigma_{\eta}M\Lambda^{-1}n\sqrt{t^{2}\Delta V}\leq n\Lambda^{-1}M\sigma_{\eta}\sqrt{V+t^{2}\Delta V}.

We see that the term
t\sigma_{\eta}\Diamond^{2}\sqrt{n}
is dominated by another term up to a constant factor, so it can be removed. This gives the final form of the bound.

∎

Appendix H
Proof of tightness

The goal of this section is to prove a constant probability upper bound on
{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\top}{{\boldsymbol{w}}_{\text{\tiny{ridge}}}}/\|{{\boldsymbol{w}}_{\text{\tiny{ridge}}}}\|_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}
for the case without label flipping noise (that is,
{\boldsymbol{y}}=\hat{{\boldsymbol{y}}}
). We are going to do it by separately bounding
S{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\top}{{\boldsymbol{w}}_{\text{\tiny{ridge}}}}
from above and
\|S{{\boldsymbol{w}}_{\text{\tiny{ridge}}}}\|_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}
from below, where
S
is the scalar from Lemma
33
. With our techniques, the bounds from below are usually more complicated then the bounds from above. This happens because of the cross-terms: one can use Cauchy-Schwarz to bound them from above, but not from below. To overcome this issue, we introduce two additional random signs to the data. More precisely, introduce two independent Rademacher random variables
\varepsilon_{y}
and
\varepsilon_{q}
, which are independent from
{\boldsymbol{y}}
and
{\boldsymbol{Q}}
, and denote

\displaystyle\bar{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}:=

\displaystyle[{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k},\varepsilon_{q}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}],

(52)

\displaystyle\bar{{\boldsymbol{y}}}:=

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{y}{\boldsymbol{y}},

(53)

\displaystyle{\bar{{\boldsymbol{w}}}_{\text{\tiny{ridge}}}}:=

\displaystyle(\bar{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}+\bar{{\boldsymbol{y}}}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\top})^{\top}(\underbrace{\bar{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}\bar{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}^{\top}+\lambda{\boldsymbol{I}}_{n}}_{={\boldsymbol{A}}})^{-1}\bar{{\boldsymbol{y}}}.

(54)

Note that since the distribution of
{\boldsymbol{y}}
is symmetric (i.e.
{\boldsymbol{y}}
and
-{\boldsymbol{y}}
have the same distribution), the distribution of
\bar{{\boldsymbol{y}}}
is the same as the distribution of
{\boldsymbol{y}}
. We are also going to assume that
{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}
is independent from
{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}
and that the distribution of
{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}
is symmetric, which implies that the
{\boldsymbol{Q}}
is has the same distribution as
\bar{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}
. Moreover, note that the expressions in the definitions of the events
\mathscr{B}_{k}(c_{B})
and
\mathscr{A}_{k}(L)
don’t change if we substitute
{\boldsymbol{Q}}
by
\bar{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}
in those definitions. Both random signs
\varepsilon_{y}
and
\varepsilon_{y}
cancel. For example, this implies Lemma
47
applies if we substitute
{\boldsymbol{Q}}
by
\bar{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}
, and its result holds almost surely over
\varepsilon_{q}
.

Introduction of those random signs allows us to say that the cross terms are non-negative with probability
0.5
independently of
{\boldsymbol{Q}}
and
{\boldsymbol{y}}
, and thus we don’t need to lower bound them to obtain results with constant probability.

By Lemma
33

\displaystyle\bar{S}:=

\displaystyle(1+\bar{{\boldsymbol{\nu}}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\bar{{\boldsymbol{y}}})^{2}+{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\top}\bar{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}_{\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\perp}}}{{\vphantom{.}\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\sim}}}}}\bar{{\boldsymbol{y}}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\bar{{\boldsymbol{y}}},

\displaystyle\bar{S}{\bar{{\boldsymbol{w}}}_{\text{\tiny{ridge}}}}=

\displaystyle(1+\bar{{\boldsymbol{\nu}}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\bar{{\boldsymbol{y}}})\bar{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\bar{{\boldsymbol{y}}}+\bar{{\boldsymbol{y}}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\bar{{\boldsymbol{y}}}\bar{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}_{\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\perp}}}{{\vphantom{.}\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\sim}}}}},

\displaystyle\bar{S}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\top}{\bar{{\boldsymbol{w}}}_{\text{\tiny{ridge}}}}=

\displaystyle\bar{{\boldsymbol{y}}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\bar{{\boldsymbol{y}}}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\top}\bar{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}_{\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\perp}}}{{\vphantom{.}\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\sim}}}}}+(1+\bar{{\boldsymbol{\nu}}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\bar{{\boldsymbol{y}}})\bar{{\boldsymbol{\nu}}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\bar{{\boldsymbol{y}}},

where we introduced
\bar{{\boldsymbol{\nu}}}:=\bar{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}
and
\bar{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}_{\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\perp}}}{{\vphantom{.}\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\sim}}}}}=({\boldsymbol{I}}_{p}-\bar{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\bar{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}){\boldsymbol{\mu}}
.

The remainder of this section is organized as follows: in Section
H.1
we bound
\bar{S}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\top}{\bar{{\boldsymbol{w}}}_{\text{\tiny{ridge}}}}
from above, in Section
H.2
we bound
\|\bar{S}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\top}{\bar{{\boldsymbol{w}}}_{\text{\tiny{ridge}}}}\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}
from below. In Section
H.3
we combine those bouds into the upper bound on
\bar{S}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\top}{\bar{{\boldsymbol{w}}}_{\text{\tiny{ridge}}}}/|\bar{S}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\top}{\bar{{\boldsymbol{w}}}_{\text{\tiny{ridge}}}}\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}
, and thus
{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\top}{{\boldsymbol{w}}_{\text{\tiny{ridge}}}}}/{\|{{\boldsymbol{w}}_{\text{\tiny{ridge}}}}\|_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}
too because it has the same distribution.

H.1
Numerator

We start with the following auxiliary lemma, which gives separate bounds on two quantities of interest that arise in the proof of the upper bound on
\bar{S}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\top}{\bar{{\boldsymbol{w}}}_{\text{\tiny{ridge}}}}
.

Lemma 49
.

Suppose that the distribution of the rows of
{\boldsymbol{Z}}
is
\sigma_{x}
-sub-Gaussian.
For any
L\geq 1
there exists a constant
c
that only depends on
L,\sigma_{x}
and
c_{B}
such that the following holds. Suppose that
k<n/c
and
{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}
is independent from
{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}
. There exists an event
\mathscr{C}
whose probability is at least
1-ce^{-n/c}
such that all the following hold on the event
\mathscr{A}_{k}(L)\cap\mathscr{B}_{k}(c_{B})\cap\mathscr{C}
:

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}\|^{2}\geq

\displaystyle c^{-1}n^{-1}\left\|\left(\Lambda n^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}\right\|^{2},

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2}\geq

\displaystyle c^{-1}\Lambda^{-2}n\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{k:\infty}}^{2}.

Proof.

We prove the inequalities separately. Recall that
c_{B}
is the constant from the definition of
\mathscr{B}_{k}(c_{B})
in Section
3.2
.

1.

Using the expressions that we derived in Section
E.2
we have on the event
\mathscr{A}_{k}(L)\cap\mathscr{B}_{k}(c_{B})

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}\left({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{-2}\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})\left\|\left({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{2}\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})}{\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{2}\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})^{2}}\left\|\left(\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})\mu_{k}({\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0:k})^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle L^{4}c_{B}^{3}n^{-1}\cdot Lc_{B}\left\|\left(\Lambda n^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}\right\|^{2}.

where we used Lemma
46
in the penultimate line.

2.

Use Sherman-Morrison-Woodbury for the matrix
{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}
(Equation (
45
)):

{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}=({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}+{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top})^{-1}={\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}-{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}({\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}+{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k})^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}^{-1}.

Let’s consider the matrix
{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}
and see what happens when we multiply it by
{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}
. The idea is to say that the column span of
{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}
is independent of
{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}
, and thus the part that lies that span doesn’t influence the norm of the vector much. Formally, denote the projector on the orthogonal complement to the span of the columns of
{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}
as
{\boldsymbol{P}}_{0:k}^{\perp}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
, and note (from Equation (
45
)) that
{\boldsymbol{P}}_{0:k}^{\perp}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}={\boldsymbol{P}}_{0:k}^{\perp}
. We write

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{-2}\|{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{-2}\|{\boldsymbol{P}}_{0:k}^{\perp}{\boldsymbol{A}}_{k}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mu_{1}({\boldsymbol{A}}_{k})^{-2}\|{\boldsymbol{P}}_{0:k}^{\perp}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2}.

Now note that the vector
{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}
has i.i.d. components, whose variances are equal to
\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2}
and whose sub-Gaussian constants don’t exceed
\sigma_{x}\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}
. Moreover,
{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}
is independent from
{\boldsymbol{P}}_{0:k}^{\perp}
, and since
{\boldsymbol{P}}_{0:k}^{\perp}
is a projector, we have

\|{\boldsymbol{P}}_{0:k}^{\perp}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2}=({\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty})^{\top}{\boldsymbol{P}}_{0:k}^{\perp}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}.

Thus, by Hanson-Wright inequality (Lemma
37
) for some absolute constant
c_{1}
and any
s>0
, the probability of the following event is at most
2\exp(-s/c_{1})

|\|{\boldsymbol{P}}_{0:k}^{\perp}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2}-\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2}\text{tr}({\boldsymbol{P}}_{0:k}^{\perp})|>\sigma_{x}^{2}\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2}\max(\sqrt{s}\|{\boldsymbol{P}}_{0:k}^{\perp}\|_{F},s\|{\boldsymbol{P}}_{0:k}^{\perp}\|).

Once again,
{\boldsymbol{P}}_{0:k}^{\perp}
is a projector of rank
n-k
, so

\text{tr}({\boldsymbol{P}}_{0:k}^{\perp})=n-k>n/2,\quad\|{\boldsymbol{P}}_{0:k}^{\perp}\|_{F}=\sqrt{n-k}\leq\sqrt{n},\quad\|{\boldsymbol{P}}_{0:k}^{\perp}\|_{F}=1.

Taking
s=n/c_{2}
for a large enough constant
c_{2}
that only depends on
\sigma_{x}
we see that the probability of the following event is at least
1-2\exp\left\{-n/(c_{1}c_{2})\right\}
:

\mathscr{C}:=\left\{\|{\boldsymbol{P}}_{0:k}^{\perp}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2}>n\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{2}-\sigma_{x}^{2}/\sqrt{c_{2}}\right)\right\}.

For
c_{2}>16\sigma_{x}^{4}
on
\mathscr{C}
we have
\|{\boldsymbol{P}}_{0:k}^{\perp}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2}\geq n\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2}/4
.

Combining everything together, on
\mathscr{A}_{k}(L)\cap\mathscr{C}
we get

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{k:\infty}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2}\geq

\displaystyle 0.25L^{-2}\Lambda^{-2}n\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2}.

∎

Our upper bound on
\bar{S}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\top}{\bar{{\boldsymbol{w}}}_{\text{\tiny{ridge}}}}
is given by the following lemma.

Lemma 50
.

Suppose that the distribution of the rows of
{\boldsymbol{Z}}
is
\sigma_{x}
-sub-Gaussian.
Consider some
L>1
. There exist large constants
a,c
that only depend on
\sigma_{x},c_{B}
and
L
and an absolute constant
c_{y}
such that the following holds. Assume that
k<n/c
and

\Lambda>cn\lambda_{k+1}\vee\sqrt{n\sum_{i>k}\lambda_{i}^{2}}.

1.

If
n\Lambda^{-1}M\geq a^{-1}\Diamond
, then on
\mathscr{A}_{k}(L)\cap\mathscr{B}_{k}(c_{B})
for any
t\in(0,\sqrt{n})
with probability at least
1-c_{y}e^{-t^{2}/c_{y}}
over the draw of
{\boldsymbol{y}}
almost surely over the draw of
(\varepsilon_{q},\varepsilon_{y})

\bar{S}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\top}{\bar{{\boldsymbol{w}}}_{\text{\tiny{ridge}}}}<c(1+t)n\Lambda^{-1}M

2.

If
n\Lambda^{-1}M<a^{-1}\Diamond
, there exists an event
\mathscr{C}
that only depends on
{\boldsymbol{Q}}
, whose probability is at least
1-ce^{-n/c}
such that then on
\mathscr{A}_{k}(L)\cap\mathscr{B}_{k}(c_{B})\cap\mathscr{C}
with probability at least
c_{y}^{-1}
over the draw of
{\boldsymbol{y}}
and
(\varepsilon_{q},\varepsilon_{y})

\bar{S}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\top}{\bar{{\boldsymbol{w}}}_{\text{\tiny{ridge}}}}<0.

Proof.

Recall that

\bar{S}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\top}{\bar{{\boldsymbol{w}}}_{\text{\tiny{ridge}}}}=\bar{{\boldsymbol{y}}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\bar{{\boldsymbol{y}}}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\top}\bar{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}_{\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\perp}}}{{\vphantom{.}\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\sim}}}}}+(1+\bar{{\boldsymbol{\nu}}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\bar{{\boldsymbol{y}}})\bar{{\boldsymbol{\nu}}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\bar{{\boldsymbol{y}}}.

First of all, denote the constant from Lemma
47
as
c_{1}
. By that lemma on
\mathscr{A}_{k}(L)\cap\mathscr{B}_{k}(c_{B})
we have

\displaystyle\bar{{\boldsymbol{y}}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\bar{{\boldsymbol{y}}}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\top}\bar{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}_{\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\perp}}}{{\vphantom{.}\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\sim}}}}}\leq

\displaystyle\mu_{n}({\boldsymbol{A}})^{-1}\|\bar{{\boldsymbol{y}}}\|^{2}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\top}\bar{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}_{\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\perp}}}{{\vphantom{.}\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\sim}}}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle L\Lambda^{-1}n\cdot c_{1}M;

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\bar{{\boldsymbol{\nu}}}\|\leq

\displaystyle c_{1}\Diamond.

Recall that we indeed can apply Lemma
47
to
\|{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\bar{{\boldsymbol{\nu}}}\|
instead of
\|{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{{\boldsymbol{\nu}}}\|
because introducing
\varepsilon_{q}
into the matrix
{\boldsymbol{Q}}
does not change the definitions of the events
\mathscr{A}_{k}(L)
and
\mathscr{B}_{k}(c_{B})
.

In the same way as in the proof of Lemma
43
since
{\boldsymbol{y}}
is a sub-Gaussian vector with sub-Gaussian norm bounded by an absolute constant, we have for some absolute constant
c_{y,1}
that for any
t>0
on
\mathscr{A}_{k}(L)
and
\mathscr{B}_{k}(c_{B})
with probability at least
1-c_{y,1}e^{-t^{2}/c_{y,1}}
over the draw of
{\boldsymbol{y}}
almost surely over the draw of
\varepsilon_{q}

|\bar{{\boldsymbol{\nu}}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\bar{{\boldsymbol{y}}}|=|\bar{{\boldsymbol{\nu}}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{y}}|\leq c_{1}t\Diamond.

We can make that statement almost surely over
\varepsilon_{q}
, because it can only take two values, so we can just do multiplicity correction by adjusting the constant
c_{y,1}
.

These upper bounds directly imply the first part of the lemma. Indeed,

\displaystyle\bar{S}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\top}{\bar{{\boldsymbol{w}}}_{\text{\tiny{ridge}}}}=

\displaystyle\bar{{\boldsymbol{y}}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\bar{{\boldsymbol{y}}}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\top}({\boldsymbol{I}}_{p}-\bar{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\bar{{\boldsymbol{Q}}})+(1+\bar{{\boldsymbol{\nu}}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\bar{{\boldsymbol{y}}})\bar{{\boldsymbol{\nu}}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\bar{{\boldsymbol{y}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{1}^{2}n\Lambda^{-1}M+c_{1}t\Diamond+(c_{1}t\Diamond)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{1}^{2}n\Lambda^{-1}M+c_{1}t\Diamond+c_{1}n\Diamond^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle n\Lambda^{-1}M(c_{1}^{2}+c_{1})+c_{1}tan\Lambda^{-1}M,

where we used that
t<\sqrt{n}
,
\Diamond^{2}\leq\Lambda^{-1}M
(Lemma
7
) and
\Diamond<an\Lambda^{-1}M
in the last line. In the end, we just need
c
to be large enough depending on
c_{1}
and
a
.

When it comes to the second part, we leave the same bounds for the terms
\bar{{\boldsymbol{y}}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\bar{{\boldsymbol{y}}}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\top}({\boldsymbol{I}}_{p}-\bar{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\bar{{\boldsymbol{Q}}})
and
(\bar{{\boldsymbol{\nu}}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\bar{{\boldsymbol{y}}})^{2}
, but show that the term
\bar{{\boldsymbol{\nu}}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\bar{{\boldsymbol{y}}}
can be negative with large enough magnitude to pull the whole bound in the negative direction.

We take the event
\mathscr{C}
to be the same as in Lemma
49
, by which there exists a constant
c_{2}
that only depends on
L,\sigma_{x},c_{B}
such that on
\mathscr{A}_{k}(L)\cap\mathscr{B}_{k}(c_{B})\cap\mathscr{C}

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{0:k}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}\|^{2}\geq
