See
28

Proof.

Note that under assumption
\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}\|=0
we have

\displaystyle M=\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2}=\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\|^{2},\quad\Diamond^{2}=n\Lambda^{-2}\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2}_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{k:\infty}}=n\Lambda^{-2}\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\|^{2}_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}},

and thus, the condition
\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\|^{2}\geq 2c\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}
can be rewritten as
M\geq 2c\sqrt{n}\Lambda\Diamond
. Therefore, it implies that
n\Lambda^{-1}M-ct\Diamond\geq n\Lambda^{-1}M/2
for
t\leq\sqrt{n}
. Thus,

\frac{n\Lambda^{-1}M-ct\Diamond}{\sqrt{V+t^{2}\Delta V}+\sqrt{n}\Diamond}\geq\frac{1}{2}\frac{\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\|^{2}}{n^{-1}\Lambda\sqrt{V+t^{2}\Delta V}+\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\|_{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}.

(56)

We see that in order to compare Equation (
56
) and (
38
), we need to compare
A+B+\lambda_{j}
to
n^{-1}\Lambda\sqrt{V+t^{2}\Delta V}

Note that

\displaystyle A\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda_{1}\Lambda}{n\lambda_{1}+\Lambda}=n^{-1}\left((n\lambda_{1})^{-1}+\Lambda^{-1}\right)^{-1}\geq\frac{1}{2n}\left(\Lambda\wedge n\lambda_{1}\right),

\displaystyle B\geq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\sum_{i\neq 1,j}\lambda_{i}^{2}},

\displaystyle V=

\displaystyle n^{-1}(1+n^{-1}\Lambda\lambda_{1}^{-1})^{-2}+\Lambda^{-2}n\sum_{i>1}\lambda_{i}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n\Lambda^{-2}\left(n^{-2}(\Lambda^{-1}+n^{-1}\lambda_{1}^{-1})^{-2}+\sum_{i>1}\lambda_{i}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle n\Lambda^{-2}(A^{2}+B^{2}+\lambda_{j}^{2}),

\displaystyle\Delta V=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\wedge\frac{n\lambda_{1}^{2}}{\Lambda^{2}}+\frac{n\lambda_{2}^{2}+\sum_{i>1}\lambda_{i}^{2}}{\Lambda^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n\Lambda^{2}}\left(\Lambda\wedge n\lambda_{1}\right)^{2}+\frac{n^{-1}(n\lambda_{2})^{2}+\sum_{i>1}\lambda_{i}^{2}}{\Lambda^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n\Lambda^{2}}\left(\Lambda\wedge n\lambda_{1}\right)^{2}+\frac{n^{-1}(n\lambda_{1}\wedge\Lambda)^{2}+\sum_{i>1}\lambda_{i}^{2}}{\Lambda^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n\Lambda^{2}}\left(2nA\right)^{2}+\frac{n^{-1}(2nA)^{2}+B^{2}+\lambda_{j}^{2}}{\Lambda^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n\Lambda^{-2}(8A^{2}+B^{2}/n+\lambda_{j}^{2}/n),

where we used that
n\lambda_{2}\leq n\lambda_{1}
and
n\lambda_{2}\leq\sum_{i>1}\lambda_{i}\leq\Lambda
for
\lambda>0
to write
n\lambda_{2}\leq n\lambda_{1}\wedge\Lambda
when we bounded
\Delta V
. Overall, we get

n^{-2}\Lambda^{2}(V+t^{2}\Delta V)\leq\frac{1}{n}(A^{2}+B^{2}+\lambda_{j}^{2})+\frac{t^{2}}{n}(8A^{2}+B^{2}/n+\lambda_{j}^{2}/n),

that is, for
t<\sqrt{n}

\displaystyle n^{-2}\Lambda^{2}(V+t^{2}\Delta V)\leq

\displaystyle 9A^{2}+2B^{2}/n+2\lambda_{j}^{2}/n,

\displaystyle n^{-1}\Lambda\sqrt{V+t^{2}\Delta V}\leq

\displaystyle 3(A+B+\lambda_{j}),

which yields the result.
∎

See
31

Proof.

Throughout the proof we treat
q,r,s
as constants and use small-oh notation
o(1)
to denote a function of
n,q,r,s
that converges to zero as
n
goes to infinity. Each time we use this notation it denotes a different function.

First of all, let’s write out the quantities of interest and plug in the expressions for
\lambda_{i}
.

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\mu}}:=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{2\lambda_{1}}{\pi}}{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1}=\sqrt{2/\pi}n^{(s-q-r)/2}{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1},

\displaystyle\Lambda=

\displaystyle\sum_{i>k}\lambda_{i}=(n^{s}-n^{r})\lambda_{k+1}=(n^{s}-n^{r})\cdot(1-n^{-q})/(1-n^{r-s})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n^{s}-n^{s-q}=n^{s}(1-o(1)),

\displaystyle\Lambda n^{-1}\lambda_{1}^{-1}=

\displaystyle n^{s}(1-o(1))\cdot n^{-1}\cdot n^{-s+q+r}=n^{q+r-1}(1-o(1)),

\displaystyle\Diamond^{2}=

\displaystyle n^{-1}\left\|\left(\Lambda n^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}\right)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}\right\|^{2}+n\Lambda^{-2}\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{k:\infty}}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n^{-1}\cdot(1+\Lambda n^{-1}\lambda_{1}^{-1})^{-2}\lambda_{1}^{-1}\cdot\frac{2\lambda_{1}}{\pi}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2+o(1)}{\pi n\left(1+n^{q+r-1}\right)^{2}},

\displaystyle n\Lambda^{-1}M=

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\Lambda n^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}\right)^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0:k}\right\|^{2}+n\Lambda^{-1}\|{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{k:\infty}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1+\Lambda n^{-1}\lambda_{1}^{-1})^{-1}\lambda_{1}^{-1}\cdot\frac{2\lambda_{1}}{\pi}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2+o(1)}{\pi(1+n^{q+r-1})},

\displaystyle V=

\displaystyle n^{-1}\text{tr}\left(\left(\Lambda n^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0:k}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{I}}_{k}\right)^{-2}\right)+\Lambda^{-2}n\sum_{i>k}\lambda_{i}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n^{-1}n^{r}(\Lambda n^{-1}\lambda_{1}^{-1}+1)^{-2}+\Lambda^{-2}n(n^{s}-n^{r})\lambda_{k+1}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1+o(1)}{n^{1-r}(1+n^{q+r-1})^{2}}+n^{1-s}(1+o(1)),

\displaystyle\Delta V=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\wedge\frac{n\lambda_{1}^{2}}{\Lambda^{2}}+\frac{n\lambda_{k+1}^{2}+\sum_{i>k}\lambda_{i}^{2}}{\Lambda^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n^{-1}\wedge n^{1-2r-2q}(1+o(1))+n^{-s}(1+o(1)).

Now let’s plug this into the quantity of interest.
Note that as long as
t=o(\sqrt{n})
, we have
t\Diamond=o(n\Lambda^{-1}M)
. Moreover,
\Delta V/V=O(n^{-r})
, indeed

n^{r}\Delta V=\left(\frac{1}{n^{1-r}\vee n^{2(q+r-1)-r}}+n^{r-s}\right)(1+o(1))\leq V(1+o(1)),

since
r<1
. Thus, if
t^{2}=o(n^{r})
, then
V+t^{2}\Delta V=V(1+o(1))
. Now note that
n\Lambda^{-1}M=\Diamond\sqrt{n}(\sqrt{2/\pi}+o(1))
. That is,

\frac{n\Lambda^{-1}M}{\sqrt{V}+\sqrt{n}\Diamond}=\frac{\sqrt{2/\pi}+o(1)}{1+\sqrt{V/(n\Diamond^{2})}}.

So, the only thing left is to compare
V
and
n\Diamond^{2}
:

\frac{V}{n\Diamond^{2}}=\frac{\pi+o(1)}{2}\left(n^{r-1}+n^{1-s}(1+n^{q+r-1})^{2}\right).

Since
r<1<s
, this ratio goes to infinity if and only if
n^{1-s}(n^{q+r-1})^{2}
goes to infinity, that is
2q+2r-1-s>0
, which yields the result.
∎
